 succeeded in 0ms:
---
name: code-review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes — Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** — does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** — does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you — run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point — a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here — not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) — fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

On top of whatever the repo documents, the Standards axis always carries the **smell baseline** below — a fixed set of Fowler code smells (_Refactoring_, ch.3) that applies even when a repo documents nothing. Two rules bind it:

- **The repo overrides.** A documented repo standard always wins; where it endorses something the baseline would flag, suppress the smell.
- **Always a judgement call.** Each smell is a labelled heuristic ("possible Feature Envy"), never a hard violation — and, like any standard here, skip anything tooling already enforces.

Each smell reads *what it is* → *how to fix*; match it against the diff:

- **Mysterious Name** — a function, variable, or type whose name doesn't reveal what it does or holds. → rename it; if no honest name comes, the design's murky.
- **Duplicated Code** — the same logic shape appears in more than one hunk or file in the change. → extract the shared shape, call it from both.
- **Feature Envy** — a method that reaches into another object's data more than its own. → move the method onto the data it envies.
- **Data Clumps** — the same few fields or params keep travelling together (a type wanting to be born). → bundle them into one type, pass that.
- **Primitive Obsession** — a primitive or string standing in for a domain concept that deserves its own type. → give the concept its own small type.
- **Repeated Switches** — the same `switch`/`if`-cascade on the same type recurs across the change. → replace with polymorphism, or one map both sites share.
- **Shotgun Surgery** — one logical change forces scattered edits across many files in the diff. → gather what changes together into one module.
- **Divergent Change** — one file or module is edited for several unrelated reasons. → split so each module changes for one reason.
- **Speculative Generality** — abstraction, parameters, or hooks added for needs the spec doesn't have. → delete it; inline back until a real need shows.
- **Message Chains** — long `a.b().c().d()` navigation the caller shouldn't depend on. → hide the walk behind one method on the first object.
- **Middle Man** — a class or function that mostly just delegates onward. → cut it, call the real target direct.
- **Refused Bequest** — a subclass or implementer that ignores or overrides most of what it inherits. → drop the inheritance, use composition.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** — include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3, **plus the smell baseline from step 3** pasted in full — the sub-agent has no other access to it.
- The brief: "Report — per file/hunk where relevant — (a) every place the diff violates a documented standard: cite the standard (file + the rule); and (b) any baseline smell you spot: name it and quote the hunk. Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls — documented-standard breaches can be hard, but baseline smells are always judgement calls, and a documented repo standard overrides the baseline. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** — include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings — the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes — that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing → **Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions → **Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

    49	## 3. Primary comparisons
    50	
    51	One primary rule: `numericTrend`, the single evidence-using proposer nominated in advance. Two primary comparisons on the test split, both on
    52	imputed multiclass Brier (abstentions, invalid and missing predictions scored with the frozen dev prior), per-family means, paired whole-family
    53	bootstrap of the difference `numericTrend` minus comparator with the protocol's resamples and seed, 95% percentile interval:
    54	
    55	- (A) against `shuffledEvidence`, its own evidence-shuffled control;
    56	- (B) against `priorMajority`.
    57	
    58	Everything else (`v0Rules`, `fixedRule`, `shuffledEvidence`, `priorMajority`, `alwaysNone`, `abstainAll` and anything added later) is descriptive
    59	and cannot produce a POSITIVE or NEGATIVE claim: `v0Rules` and `fixedRule` choose from the operation kind alone, so shuffling evidence cannot
    60	change them and beating `priorMajority` would not show evidence use. `mechanismRule` is a dev oracle and is not evaluated on test. One named rule
    61	with a conjunction of two comparisons means no across-rule multiplicity correction is needed or claimed. Secondary, descriptive: directional
    62	accuracy, coverage, invalid counts, the trend-confound diagnostic, label agreement with fixture expectations, the measurement-limited share.
    63	
    64	Stated limitation: the shuffled control for case i is another family's evidence from the cyclic donor map, so per-family differences in (A) are
    65	mildly dependent and the independent-family bootstrap is nominal for (A); this amendment does not claim calibrated coverage for (A). POSITIVE
    66	requires (B) as well, which has no donor coupling.
    67	
    68	## 4. Reading, applied mechanically, in this precedence
    69	
    70	The reading is the pure function `confirmReading` on the committed `ledger.jsonl` (one row per test case per non-oracle proposer) and the
    71	parameters in `confirmation.json`; the delivered-output test recomputes it from the ledger.
    72	
    73	a. **UNINFORMATIVE (override)** if any of: fewer than 10 test families; `meta.json` status is not `CONFIRM`; either primary interval is null or
    74	   has a non-finite endpoint; the paired family or case sets of `numericTrend` and a comparator are not identical; `numericTrend` has any invalid
    75	   or missing prediction on the test split; `numericTrend` answers zero test cases or predicts one class on all its answered test cases
    76	   (degenerate); `priorMajority` or `shuffledEvidence` has any invalid or missing prediction (they are exempt from the degenerate check:
    77	   `priorMajority` is constant by design); measurement-limited cases exceed 25% of test cases; provenance is not verified (see below). An override
    78	   beats NEGATIVE and POSITIVE.
    79	b. **NEGATIVE** iff some primary interval lies wholly above 0 (supported inferiority of `numericTrend`).
    80	c. **POSITIVE** iff both primary intervals lie wholly below 0.
    81	d. Otherwise **UNINFORMATIVE**, with the descriptive suffix "no improvement shown" when the point estimate against `priorMajority` is at least 0,
    82	   else "improvement not supported". The suffix does not change the label; a nonnegative point estimate alone is never NEGATIVE.
    83	
    84	## 5. Meaning
    85	
    86	- POSITIVE: on these fixtures `numericTrend` beat its shuffled control and the prior out of sample. Not evidence for any model, production world
    87	  or the naturalist feature.
    88	- NEGATIVE: `numericTrend` is worse than a comparator with interval support.
    89	- UNINFORMATIVE: the fixtures or counts could not decide; no claim.
    90	- v0 exploratory numbers are not reused as evidence and are never merged into one table with these.
    91	
    92	Status is earned, not read from `meta.json`. `confirm-analysis` first requires the data files to match `canonical.sha256` and the envelopes and
    93	outcome rows to equal the case inventory the protocol implies (every family times every mechanism): a missing family is an error, never a
    94	smaller ledger. It then withholds `CONFIRM` (the document status becomes `UNVERIFIED`, which is the same override as a non-CONFIRM status) unless
    95	`meta.json` records an amended confirm-stage run that started with a clean closure, its snapshot is this recipe's protocol, and the committed
    96	(HEAD) amendment, protocol and freeze match the recorded hashes. No stage other than the amended confirm run may simulate a protocol that uses the
    97	reserved salt or seeds (smoke and dev refuse, renamed snapshots included).
    98	
    99	## 6. Reporting and attempts
   100	
   101	- Failed predictions are reported first and in full (RESULT.md, `report.html`); no post-hoc relabelling, threshold change, family exclusion or
   102	  reinterpretation. Deviations go in a dated addendum below, never an edit of earlier text.
   103	- The first completed run is the attempt of record and stays committed. Replays for reproducibility go to `/tmp` only and are compared by digest.
   104	  If the run fails or a scoring bug is found: stop, record it in `docs/prediction-confirm-2026-11-02/BUG.md`, keep partial output untouched. A
   105	  rerun on changed code needs a new dated amendment AND a newly drawn disjoint family set (new salt and seed range), because these families
   106	  would have been seen. A crash before any outcome is written (for example out of memory) may be rerun unchanged on the same families.
   107	- `envelopes.jsonl` and `predictions.jsonl` are gitignored like v0, with digests in `canonical.sha256`; `confirm.sha256` seals `confirmation.json`
   108	  and `ledger.jsonl`.
   109	
   110	## Limits
   111	
   112	20 fixture families of 32x32 CPU worlds, one rising recipe; saturated worlds push labels toward "none"; the intervals are descriptive at this n;
   113	the shuffled-control contrast has donor-coupled dependence; label agreement with expectations is reported, never forced.

    45	
    46	Positive differences mean `numericTrend` is worse. (B) is wholly above 0, which is what the NEGATIVE rule requires.
    47	
    48	## What this establishes and what it does not
    49	
    50	Establishes: on these 16 held-out v0 CPU fixture families, `numericTrend` scored worse than the constant prior (`priorMajority`) on multiclass Brier, with interval support.
    51	Its probabilities are overconfident and mostly point down while most outcomes are `none`. Comparison (A) does not separate it from its own shuffled-evidence control (the interval spans 0 and the
    52	point estimate is slightly positive), so this run shows no evidence that `numericTrend` uses its evidence usefully. The two-class pattern (78 down, 18 up) was the same for the shuffled control.
    53	
    54	Does not establish: anything about any model, GPU world, production preset or the naturalist feature; why `numericTrend` fails (no mechanism claim was tested); that no evidence-using rule could succeed.
    55	The descriptive proposers (`v0Rules` had the lowest Brier, 0.437) cannot produce a POSITIVE or NEGATIVE claim under the amendment and are not read as one; they choose from the operation kind alone.
    56	Limits as in the amendment: 20 fixture families of 32x32 CPU worlds, one rising recipe, donor-coupled dependence in (A) so its bootstrap is nominal, intervals descriptive at this n.
    57	The v0 exploratory numbers are not merged with these. No post-hoc reinterpretation, family exclusion or threshold change was made.
    58

     1	# I02 evidence: what the harness run showed, and what it did not
     2	
     3	Slice S2, branch `claude/i02-prediction-envelope`, 2026-11-01. CPU only (sim-ref); no model call, no GPU, no network, no paid
     4	compute. Outputs: `experiments/prediction-envelope/v0/` (see `comparison.md`, `report.html`, `meta.json`); regenerated 2026-11-01 with the rising cohort (Deviation 3). No advance or stop
     5	decision about any model is evaluable from this run.
     6	
     7	**Update 2026-11-02 (I02b):** a held-out confirmation on 20 fresh families was run and read mechanically: **NEGATIVE** for `numericTrend` (worse than `priorMajority`, interval above 0; not separable from its shuffled control). See `docs/prediction-confirm-2026-11-02/RESULT.md` and `experiments/prediction-envelope/confirm-2026-11-02/report.html`. The v0 text above is unchanged.
     8	
     9	## Status of the delivered run: EXPLORATORY, not a held-out confirmation
    10	
    11	`meta.json` says `status: "EXPLORATORY"`. The first delivered run (superseded) generated test-split outcomes from a working-tree

     1	// Scoring (PLAN.md, section 8). Multiclass Brier over {up, down, none}: BS = (1/N) sum_i sum_c (p_ic/1000 - o_ic)^2, range 0..2.
     2	// The headline imputes abstentions and invalid/missing predictions with the frozen dev-split prior, so abstaining cannot beat the
     3	// prior; answered-only numbers are reported beside it. A calibrated majority baseline is legitimately strong and is not claimed
     4	// to be punished. The family is the unit of uncertainty.
     5	import { mulberry32 } from "@bl/metrics";
     6	import { CLASSES, type Direction3 } from "./envelope.ts";
     7	import { argmax, type Prediction, type Probs } from "./validate.ts";
     8	
     9	export interface ScoredCase {
    10	  caseId: string;
    11	  familyId: string;
    12	  outcome: Direction3;
    13	  /** null: abstained, invalid or missing. */
    14	  prediction: Prediction | null;
    15	  status: "answered" | "abstained" | "invalid" | "missing";
    16	}
    17	
    18	export const brierOf = (p: Probs, o: Direction3): number => {
    19	  let s = 0;
    20	  for (const c of CLASSES) {
    21	    const d = p[c] / 1000 - (c === o ? 1 : 0);
    22	    s += d * d;
    23	  }
    24	  return s;
    25	};
    26	
    27	export interface Reliability {
    28	  bin: string;
    29	  n: number;
    30	  meanStated: number | null;
    31	  hitRate: number | null;
    32	}
    33	export interface ProposerScore {
    34	  id: string;
    35	  n: number;
    36	  answered: number;
    37	  abstained: number;
    38	  invalid: number;
    39	  missing: number;
    40	  coverage: number;
    41	  abstentionRate: number;
    42	  /** Headline: abstained/invalid/missing imputed with the dev prior. */
    43	  brierImputed: number | null;
    44	  brierAnswered: number | null;
    45	  directionalAccuracy: number | null;
    46	  balancedAccuracy: number | null;
    47	  /** Classes with at least one answered case (the balanced accuracy's denominator). */
    48	  balancedClasses: number;
    49	  reliability: Reliability[];
    50	  validNonNullYield: number | null;
    51	}
    52	
    53	export function reliabilityTable(cases: readonly ScoredCase[]): Reliability[] {
    54	  const bins = [0, 0, 0, 0, 0].map(() => ({ n: 0, stated: 0, hit: 0 }));
    55	  for (const c of cases) {
    56	    if (c.status !== "answered" || !c.prediction?.probs) continue;
    57	    const a = argmax(c.prediction.probs);
    58	    const p = c.prediction.probs[a] / 1000;
    59	    const b = Math.min(4, Math.floor(p * 5));
    60	    bins[b].n++;
    61	    bins[b].stated += p;
    62	    if (a === c.outcome) bins[b].hit++;
    63	  }
    64	  return bins.map((b, i) => ({ bin: `${(i * 0.2).toFixed(1)}-${i === 4 ? "1.0" : ((i + 1) * 0.2).toFixed(1)}`, n: b.n, meanStated: b.n ? b.stated / b.n : null, hitRate: b.n ? b.hit / b.n : null }));
    65	}
    66	
    67	export function scoreProposer(id: string, cases: readonly ScoredCase[], devPrior: Probs, restrictTo?: ReadonlySet<string>): ProposerScore {
    68	  const cs = restrictTo ? cases.filter((c) => restrictTo.has(c.caseId)) : cases;
    69	  const n = cs.length;
    70	  const answered = cs.filter((c) => c.status === "answered");
    71	  let impSum = 0, ansSum = 0, hit = 0;
    72	  for (const c of cs) impSum += c.status === "answered" ? brierOf(c.prediction!.probs!, c.outcome) : brierOf(devPrior, c.outcome);
    73	  for (const c of answered) {
    74	    ansSum += brierOf(c.prediction!.probs!, c.outcome);
    75	    if (argmax(c.prediction!.probs!) === c.outcome) hit++;
    76	  }
    77	  let recSum = 0, recK = 0;
    78	  for (const k of CLASSES) {
    79	    const rows = answered.filter((c) => c.outcome === k);
    80	    if (!rows.length) continue;
    81	    recK++;
    82	    recSum += rows.filter((c) => argmax(c.prediction!.probs!) === k).length / rows.length;
    83	  }
    84	  return {
    85	    id,
    86	    n,
    87	    answered: answered.length,
    88	    abstained: cs.filter((c) => c.status === "abstained").length,
    89	    invalid: cs.filter((c) => c.status === "invalid").length,
    90	    missing: cs.filter((c) => c.status === "missing").length,
    91	    coverage: n ? answered.length / n : 0,
    92	    abstentionRate: n ? cs.filter((c) => c.status === "abstained").length / n : 0,
    93	    brierImputed: n ? impSum / n : null,
    94	    brierAnswered: answered.length ? ansSum / answered.length : null,
    95	    directionalAccuracy: answered.length ? hit / answered.length : null,
    96	    balancedAccuracy: recK ? recSum / recK : null,
    97	    balancedClasses: recK,
    98	    reliability: reliabilityTable(cs),
    99	    validNonNullYield: n ? answered.filter((c) => c.outcome !== "none").length / n : null,
   100	  };
   125	  return { n: a.length, agree, rate: a.length ? agree / a.length : null };
   126	}
   127	
   128	export interface Interval {
   129	  mean: number;
   130	  lo: number;
   131	  hi: number;
   132	}
   133	export const MIN_FAMILIES_FOR_INTERVALS = 10;
   134	
   135	/** Per-family mean of a per-case value. */
   136	export function familyMeans(rows: readonly { familyId: string; v: number }[]): Map<string, number> {
   137	  const acc = new Map<string, { s: number; n: number }>();
   138	  for (const r of rows) {
   139	    const a = acc.get(r.familyId) ?? { s: 0, n: 0 };
   140	    a.s += r.v;
   141	    a.n++;
   142	    acc.set(r.familyId, a);
   143	  }
   144	  return new Map([...acc].map(([k, a]) => [k, a.s / a.n]));
   145	}
   146	
   147	/** Whole-family bootstrap of the mean of per-family values; null below MIN_FAMILIES_FOR_INTERVALS families (descriptive only). */
   148	export function familyBootstrap(perFamily: ReadonlyMap<string, number>, resamples: number, seed: number): Interval | null {
   149	  const v = [...perFamily.keys()].sort().map((k) => perFamily.get(k)!);
   150	  if (v.length < MIN_FAMILIES_FOR_INTERVALS) return null;
   151	  const rng = mulberry32(seed);
   152	  const means: number[] = [];
   153	  for (let r = 0; r < resamples; r++) {
   154	    let s = 0;
   155	    for (let i = 0; i < v.length; i++) s += v[Math.floor(rng() * v.length)];
   156	    means.push(s / v.length);
   157	  }
   158	  means.sort((a, b) => a - b);
   159	  return { mean: v.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0) / v.length, lo: means[Math.floor(0.025 * resamples)], hi: means[Math.min(resamples - 1, Math.ceil(0.975 * resamples) - 1)] };
   160	}
   161	
   162	/** Paired per-family difference (a - b) of two per-family maps over the families both have. */
   163	export function pairedFamilyDiff(a: ReadonlyMap<string, number>, b: ReadonlyMap<string, number>): Map<string, number> {
   164	  const out = new Map<string, number>();
   165	  for (const [k, x] of a) if (b.has(k)) out.set(k, x - b.get(k)!);
   166	  return out;
   167	}
   225	export interface ProvenanceInput {
   226	  meta: { stage?: string; status?: string; protocolSha256?: string; gitDirty?: boolean; amendment?: { sha256?: string } };
   227	  snapshot: Protocol;
   228	  /** HEAD blob texts of the amendment, the confirm protocol and its freeze file (null when HEAD has none). */
   229	  committed: { amendment: string | null; protocol: string | null; freeze: string | null };
   230	}
   231	/** Empty when the run is an amended confirm run on the reserved fresh families, whose amendment, protocol and freeze are committed and consistent. */
   232	export function provenanceProblems(i: ProvenanceInput): string[] {
   233	  const out: string[] = [];
   234	  const m = i.meta;
   235	  if (m.stage !== "confirm" || m.status !== "CONFIRM") out.push(`meta.json is not a confirm-stage CONFIRM run (stage ${m.stage}, status ${m.status})`);
   236	  if (!m.amendment) out.push("meta.json carries no amendment provenance (a default v0 confirm run is not the amended confirmation)");
   237	  if (m.gitDirty !== false) out.push("the run started with uncommitted changes in the executable closure");
   238	  const hash = protocolHash(i.snapshot);
   239	  if (m.protocolSha256 !== hash) out.push("meta.protocolSha256 does not match the protocol snapshot");
   240	  if (i.snapshot.salt !== CONFIRM_SALT || canonical(i.snapshot.families) !== canonical(freshFamilies())) out.push("the protocol snapshot is not the reserved fresh-family recipe");
   241	  if (i.committed.freeze === null) out.push("the confirm freeze file is not committed");
   242	  else if (i.committed.freeze.trim() !== hash) out.push("the committed confirm freeze hash does not match the snapshot");
   243	  if (i.committed.protocol === null) out.push("the confirm protocol is not committed");
   244	  else {
   245	    try {
   246	      if (protocolHash(JSON.parse(i.committed.protocol) as Protocol) !== hash) out.push("the committed confirm protocol does not match the snapshot");
   247	    } catch {
   248	      out.push("the committed confirm protocol is unreadable");
   249	    }
   250	  }
   251	  if (i.committed.amendment === null) out.push("the amendment is not committed");
   252	  else if (!m.amendment || sha256Text(i.committed.amendment) !== m.amendment.sha256) out.push("the committed amendment does not match meta.amendment.sha256");
   253	  return out;
   254	}
   255	
   256	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
   257	// The ledger: the committed, sufficient scoring input (test split, every non-oracle proposer).
   258	
   259	export type LedgerStatus = "answered" | "abstained" | "invalid" | "missing";
   260	export interface LedgerRow {
   265	  predicted: Direction3 | null;
   266	  probs: Probs | null;
   267	  observed: Direction3;
   268	  expected: Direction3;
   269	  measurementLimited: boolean;
   270	}
   271	
   272	export function buildLedger(p: Protocol, envelopes: readonly PredictionEnvelope[], lines: readonly PredictionLine[], rows: readonly CaseRow[]): LedgerRow[] {
   273	  const minEffect = p.minEffect!;
   274	  const cover = coverageProblems(p, envelopes, rows);
   275	  if (cover.length) throw new Error(`the run does not cover the frozen case inventory: ${cover.join("; ")}`);
   276	  const test = rows.filter((r) => r.split === "test");
   277	  const ids = new Set(test.map((r) => r.caseId));
   278	  const envs = envelopes.filter((e) => ids.has(e.caseId));
   279	  const byRow = new Map(test.map((r) => [r.caseId, r]));
   280	  const out: LedgerRow[] = [];
   281	  for (const pr of buildProposers(p, envelopes).filter((x) => x.role !== "oracle")) {
   282	    const scored = scoredCases(lines, pr.id, envs, test, minEffect).sort((a, b) => (a.caseId < b.caseId ? -1 : 1));
   283	    for (const c of scored) {
   284	      const r = byRow.get(c.caseId)!;
   285	      out.push({
   286	        familyId: c.familyId,
   287	        caseId: c.caseId,
   288	        proposer: pr.id,
   289	        status: c.status,
   290	        predicted: c.status === "answered" ? argmax(c.prediction!.probs!) : null,
   291	        probs: c.status === "answered" ? c.prediction!.probs! : null,
   292	        observed: labelOf(r.effect.bodyMass, minEffect),
   293	        expected: r.expected,
   294	        measurementLimited: measurementLimited(r.effect.bodyMass, minEffect),
   295	      });
   296	    }
   297	  }
   298	  return out;
   299	}
   300	
   301	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
   302	// The reading (AMENDMENT.md section 4), applied mechanically to the ledger alone.
   303	
   304	export const PRIMARY_RULE = "numericTrend";
   305	export const COMPARATORS = { vsShuffledEvidence: "shuffledEvidence", vsPriorMajority: "priorMajority" } as const;
   306	export const MAX_MEASUREMENT_LIMITED_SHARE = 0.25;
   307	
   308	export interface ReadingParams {
   309	  devPrior: Probs;
   310	  resamples: number;
   311	  seed: number;
   312	  /** meta.json status: only CONFIRM can be decisive. */
   313	  status: string;
   314	  /** Provenance problems found by provenanceProblems(); each is an override. */
   315	  provenance?: readonly string[];
   316	}
   317	export interface PairedResult {
   318	  comparator: string;
   319	  /** numericTrend minus comparator, imputed multiclass Brier, per-family means; negative favours numericTrend. */
   320	  interval: Interval | null;
   321	  nFamilies: number;
   322	  pairingComplete: boolean;
   323	}
   324	export type Label = "POSITIVE" | "NEGATIVE" | "UNINFORMATIVE";
   325	export interface Reading {
   326	  label: Label;
   327	  /** Descriptive suffix of an UNINFORMATIVE result from rule 4d; it never changes the label. */
   328	  suffix: string | null;
   329	  /** Rule 4a overrides that fired (empty when none). */
   330	  overrides: string[];
   331	  testFamilies: number;
   332	  testCases: number;
   333	  measurementLimitedShare: number | null;
   334	  comparisons: { vsShuffledEvidence: PairedResult; vsPriorMajority: PairedResult };
   335	  basis: string;
   336	}
   337	
   338	const imputedBrier = (r: LedgerRow, prior: Probs): number => (r.status === "answered" ? brierOf(r.probs!, r.observed) : brierOf(prior, r.observed));
   339	const rowsOf = (ledger: readonly LedgerRow[], proposer: string): LedgerRow[] => ledger.filter((r) => r.proposer === proposer);
   340	const sameSet = (a: Iterable<string>, b: Iterable<string>): boolean => {
   341	  const x = [...new Set(a)].sort(), y = [...new Set(b)].sort();
   342	  return x.length === y.length && x.every((v, i) => v === y[i]);
   343	};
   344	
   345	function paired(ledger: readonly LedgerRow[], comparator: string, prm: ReadingParams): PairedResult {
   346	  const nt = rowsOf(ledger, PRIMARY_RULE), cmp = rowsOf(ledger, comparator);
   347	  const pairingComplete = nt.length > 0 && nt.length === cmp.length && sameSet(nt.map((r) => r.familyId), cmp.map((r) => r.familyId)) && sameSet(nt.map((r) => `${r.familyId}|${r.caseId}`), cmp.map((r) => `${r.familyId}|${r.caseId}`));
   348	  const mean = (rs: LedgerRow[]) => familyMeans(rs.map((r) => ({ familyId: r.familyId, v: imputedBrier(r, prm.devPrior) })));
   349	  const diff = pairedFamilyDiff(mean(nt), mean(cmp));
   350	  return { comparator, interval: familyBootstrap(diff, prm.resamples, prm.seed), nFamilies: diff.size, pairingComplete };
   351	}
   352	
   353	const finite = (v: Interval | null): v is Interval => v !== null && Number.isFinite(v.lo) && Number.isFinite(v.hi) && Number.isFinite(v.mean);
   354	
   355	export function confirmReading(ledger: readonly LedgerRow[], prm: ReadingParams): Reading {
   356	  const nt = rowsOf(ledger, PRIMARY_RULE);
   357	  const families = new Set(nt.map((r) => r.familyId));
   358	  const A = paired(ledger, COMPARATORS.vsShuffledEvidence, prm), B = paired(ledger, COMPARATORS.vsPriorMajority, prm);
   359	  const cases = new Set(nt.map((r) => r.caseId));
   360	  const limited = new Set(nt.filter((r) => r.measurementLimited).map((r) => r.caseId));
   361	  const share = cases.size ? limited.size / cases.size : null;
   362	  const overrides: string[] = [];
   363	  if (families.size < MIN_FAMILIES_FOR_INTERVALS) overrides.push(`fewer than ${MIN_FAMILIES_FOR_INTERVALS} test families (${families.size})`);
   364	  if (prm.status !== "CONFIRM") overrides.push(`meta status is ${prm.status}, not CONFIRM`);
   365	  for (const q of prm.provenance ?? []) overrides.push(`provenance: ${q}`);
   366	  for (const r of [A, B]) {
   367	    if (!finite(r.interval)) overrides.push(`the interval vs ${r.comparator} is null or not finite`);
   368	    if (!r.pairingComplete) overrides.push(`the paired family or case sets of ${PRIMARY_RULE} and ${r.comparator} are not identical`);
   369	  }
   370	  const bad = nt.filter((r) => r.status === "invalid" || r.status === "missing").length;
   371	  if (bad > 0) overrides.push(`${PRIMARY_RULE} has ${bad} invalid or missing predictions`);
   372	  const answered = nt.filter((r) => r.status === "answered");
   373	  const classes = new Set(answered.map((r) => r.predicted));
   374	  if (answered.length === 0) overrides.push(`${PRIMARY_RULE} answered no test case`);
   375	  else if (classes.size === 1) overrides.push(`${PRIMARY_RULE} is degenerate: one predicted class (${[...classes][0]}) on all ${answered.length} answered test cases`);
   376	  for (const c of Object.values(COMPARATORS)) {
   377	    const badC = rowsOf(ledger, c).filter((r) => r.status === "invalid" || r.status === "missing").length;
   378	    if (badC > 0) overrides.push(`${c} has ${badC} invalid or missing predictions`);
   379	  }
   380	  if (share !== null && share > MAX_MEASUREMENT_LIMITED_SHARE) overrides.push(`measurement-limited cases are ${(share * 100).toFixed(1)}% of test cases (limit ${MAX_MEASUREMENT_LIMITED_SHARE * 100}%)`);
   381	  const base = { suffix: null as string | null, testFamilies: families.size, testCases: cases.size, measurementLimitedShare: share, comparisons: { vsShuffledEvidence: A, vsPriorMajority: B } };
   382	  if (overrides.length) return { ...base, label: "UNINFORMATIVE", overrides, basis: "rule 4a: an override fired" };
   383	  const a = A.interval!, b = B.interval!;
   384	  if (a.lo > 0 || b.lo > 0) return { ...base, label: "NEGATIVE", overrides, basis: "rule 4b: a primary interval lies wholly above 0" };
   385	  if (a.hi < 0 && b.hi < 0) return { ...base, label: "POSITIVE", overrides, basis: "rule 4c: both primary intervals lie wholly below 0" };
   386	  return { ...base, label: "UNINFORMATIVE", overrides, suffix: b.mean >= 0 ? "no improvement shown" : "improvement not supported", basis: "rule 4d: neither NEGATIVE nor POSITIVE" };
   387	}
   388	
   389	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
   390	// The sealed decision document.
   391	
   392	export interface DescriptiveRow {
   393	  proposer: string;
   394	  n: number;
   395	  answered: number;
   425	  /** numericTrend's failed predictions on the test split (wrong argmax, invalid or missing); abstentions are not failures. */
   426	  failedPredictions: FailedPrediction[];
   427	}
   428	
   429	export function describeLedger(ledger: readonly LedgerRow[], prior: Probs): DescriptiveRow[] {
   430	  const ids = [...new Set(ledger.map((r) => r.proposer))];
   431	  return ids.map((id) => {
   432	    const rs = rowsOf(ledger, id);
   433	    const ans = rs.filter((r) => r.status === "answered");
   434	    const predictedClasses: Record<Direction3, number> = { up: 0, down: 0, none: 0 };
   435	    for (const r of ans) predictedClasses[r.predicted!]++;
   436	    return {
   437	      proposer: id,
   438	      n: rs.length,
   439	      answered: ans.length,
   440	      abstained: rs.filter((r) => r.status === "abstained").length,
   441	      invalid: rs.filter((r) => r.status === "invalid").length,
   442	      missing: rs.filter((r) => r.status === "missing").length,
   443	      coverage: rs.length ? ans.length / rs.length : 0,
   444	      brierImputed: rs.length ? rs.reduce((s, r) => s + imputedBrier(r, prior), 0) / rs.length : null,
   445	      directionalAccuracy: ans.length ? ans.filter((r) => r.predicted === r.observed).length / ans.length : null,
   446	      predictedClasses,
   447	      labelAgreementWithExpectation: ans.length ? ans.filter((r) => r.predicted === r.expected).length / ans.length : null,
   448	    };
   449	  });
   450	}
   451	
   452	export function buildConfirmation(p: Protocol, metaStatus: string, ledger: readonly LedgerRow[], provenance: readonly string[] = []): ConfirmationDoc {
   453	  // CONFIRM is earned: any provenance problem withholds it.
   454	  const status = provenance.length ? "UNVERIFIED" : metaStatus;
   455	  const prm: ReadingParams = { devPrior: p.devPrior!, resamples: p.bootstrap.resamples, seed: p.bootstrap.seed, status, provenance };
   456	  const failedPredictions = rowsOf(ledger, PRIMARY_RULE)
   457	    .filter((r) => r.status === "invalid" || r.status === "missing" || (r.status === "answered" && r.predicted !== r.observed))
   458	    .map((r) => ({ caseId: r.caseId, familyId: r.familyId, status: r.status, predicted: r.predicted, observed: r.observed }));
   459	  return {
   460	    kind: "prediction-confirmation",
   461	    version: 1,
   462	    status,
   463	    protocolSha256: sha256Text(canonical(p)),
   464	    primaryRule: PRIMARY_RULE,
   465	    params: { resamples: prm.resamples, seed: prm.seed, devPrior: prm.devPrior, minFamilies: MIN_FAMILIES_FOR_INTERVALS, maxMeasurementLimitedShare: MAX_MEASUREMENT_LIMITED_SHARE },
   466	    reading: confirmReading(ledger, prm),
   467	    provenanceProblems: [...provenance],
   468	    descriptive: describeLedger(ledger, prm.devPrior),
   469	    failedPredictions,
   470	  };
   471	}
   472	
   473	/** The sha256 file over the sealed decision and the ledger. */
   474	export const confirmDigest = (confirmationText: string, ledgerText: string): string => `${sha256Text(confirmationText)}  confirmation.json\n${sha256Text(ledgerText)}  ledger.jsonl\n`;
     1	// The transitive local source closure of an entry file: every repo file reachable through relative imports and `@bl/*` aliases.
     2	// meta.json's source hash covers all of it, so a change to the simulator, schema, metrics or a helper the harness runs changes the hash.
     3	import { existsSync, readFileSync } from "node:fs";
     4	import { dirname, join, relative, resolve } from "node:path";
     5	import { sha256Text } from "../propose.ts";
     6	
     7	const SPEC = /(?:^|\n)\s*(?:import|export)\s[^"'`;]*?from\s*["']([^"']+)["']|(?:^|\n)\s*import\s*["']([^"']+)["']|\bimport\(\s*["']([^"']+)["']\s*\)/g;
     8	
     9	/** Resolves an import specifier from `fromFile` to a repo file, or null for an external/builtin module. */
    10	export function resolveLocal(root: string, fromFile: string, spec: string): string | null {
    11	  let base: string;
    12	  if (spec.startsWith("./") || spec.startsWith("../")) base = resolve(dirname(fromFile), spec);
    13	  else if (spec.startsWith("@bl/")) base = join(root, "packages", spec.slice(4), "src/index.ts");
    14	  else return null;
    15	  for (const c of [base, base + ".ts", join(base, "index.ts")]) if (existsSync(c) && /\.(ts|js|mjs)$/.test(c)) return c;
    16	  throw new Error(`cannot resolve ${JSON.stringify(spec)} from ${relative(root, fromFile)}`);
    17	}
    18	
    19	/** Repo-relative paths of the closure, sorted. */
    20	export function sourceClosure(root: string, entries: readonly string[]): string[] {
    21	  const seen = new Set<string>();
    22	  const stack = entries.map((e) => resolve(root, e));
    23	  while (stack.length) {
    24	    const f = stack.pop()!;
    25	    if (seen.has(f)) continue;
    26	    seen.add(f);
    27	    const text = readFileSync(f, "utf8");
    28	    for (const m of text.matchAll(SPEC)) {
    29	      const spec = m[1] ?? m[2] ?? m[3];
    30	      const r = spec ? resolveLocal(root, f, spec) : null;
    31	      if (r && !seen.has(r)) stack.push(r);
    32	    }
    33	  }
    34	  return [...seen].map((f) => relative(root, f)).sort();
    35	}
    36	
    37	/** Resolver configuration and dependency identities that decide what a specifier means: hashed alongside the sources. */
    38	export const RESOLVER_CONFIG_FILES = ["deno.json", "package.json", "tsconfig.json"] as const;
    39	
    40	/** Bare (non-local) specifiers the closure imports, e.g. `node:fs` or `fflate`: recorded so a silently skipped external is still named. */
    41	export function externalSpecifiers(root: string, files: readonly string[]): string[] {
    42	  const out = new Set<string>();
    43	  for (const f of files) {
    44	    for (const m of readFileSync(join(root, f), "utf8").matchAll(SPEC)) {
    45	      const spec = m[1] ?? m[2] ?? m[3];
    46	      if (spec && !spec.startsWith("./") && !spec.startsWith("../") && !spec.startsWith("@bl/")) out.add(spec);
    47	    }
    48	  }
    49	  return [...out].sort();
    50	}
    51	
    52	/**
    53	 * The hash covers the closure's files, the resolver configuration (deno.json import map and npm pins, package.json, tsconfig.json
    54	 * paths) and the sorted list of external specifiers, so changing what `@bl/*` or `fflate` resolves to changes the hash.
    55	 */
    56	export function sourceClosureHash(root: string, entries: readonly string[]): { sha256: string; files: string[]; config: string[]; externals: string[] } {
    57	  const files = sourceClosure(root, entries);
    58	  const config = RESOLVER_CONFIG_FILES.filter((c) => existsSync(join(root, c)));
    59	  const externals = externalSpecifiers(root, files);
    60	  const parts = [...files, ...config].map((f) => `${f}\n${sha256Text(readFileSync(join(root, f), "utf8"))}`);
    61	  return { sha256: sha256Text([...parts, `externals\n${externals.join(",")}`].join("\n")), files, config, externals };
    62	}
    45	}
    46	
    47	function args(argv: string[]): { cmd: string; pos: string[]; opt: Record<string, string> } {
    48	  const [cmd = "", ...rest] = argv;
    49	  const pos: string[] = [], opt: Record<string, string> = {};
    50	  for (let i = 0; i < rest.length; i++) {
    51	    if (rest[i].startsWith("--")) {
    52	      opt[rest[i].slice(2)] = rest[i + 1] ?? "";
    53	      i++;
    54	    } else pos.push(rest[i]);
    55	  }
    56	  return { cmd, pos, opt };
    57	}
    58	const readProtocol = (path: string): Protocol => JSON.parse(readFileSync(path, "utf8")) as Protocol;
    59	const writeText = (path: string, text: string): void => {
    60	  mkdirSync(dirname(path), { recursive: true });
    61	  writeFileSync(path, text);
    62	};
    63	function sh(cmd: string, a: string[]): string {
    64	  try {
    65	    return execFileSync(cmd, a, { cwd: root, encoding: "utf8", stdio: ["ignore", "pipe", "ignore"] }).trim();
    66	  } catch {
    67	    return "unknown";
    68	  }
    69	}
    70	const CLOSURE_ENTRIES = ["tools/predict.ts", "tools/lib/prediction/model-seam.ts"];
    71	const sourceClosureHash = (): string => closureHash(root, CLOSURE_ENTRIES).sha256;
    72	/** The committed (HEAD) text of a repo path, or null when HEAD has no such file. */
    73	function committedText(path: string): string | null {
    74	  try {
    75	    return execFileSync("git", ["show", `HEAD:${relative(root, path)}`], { cwd: root, encoding: "utf8", stdio: ["ignore", "pipe", "ignore"] });
   112	    const amended = stage === "confirm" && opt.protocol !== undefined;
   113	    if (stage === "dev" && opt.protocol !== undefined) throw new Error("the dev stage uses the v0 protocol only");
   114	    const protoPath = stage === "smoke" ? resolve(opt.protocol ?? "") : amended ? resolve(opt.protocol) : PROTOCOL;
   115	    const freezePath = amended ? resolve(opt.freeze ?? "") : FREEZE;
   116	    if (amended && (!opt.freeze || !opt.amendment)) throw new Error("--protocol for the confirm stage needs --freeze and --amendment");
   117	    const amendmentPath = amended ? resolve(opt.amendment) : "";
   118	    const p = readProtocol(protoPath);
   119	    const t0 = Date.now();
   120	    // The reserved confirmation families may be simulated only by the amended confirm run, whose gate demands the committed amendment.
   121	    if (!amended) {
   122	      const reserved = reservedConfirmProblems(p);
   123	      if (reserved.length) throw new Error(`refusing to run stage ${stage}: the protocol uses reserved held-out confirmation identities (${reserved.slice(0, 3).join("; ")}); only \`run --stage confirm --protocol ... --amendment ...\` on committed files may simulate them`);
   124	    }
   125	    // Source state is captured before generation and before any output is written.
   126	    const dirtyAtStart = closureStatus();
   127	    const gitShaAtStart = sh("git", ["rev-parse", "HEAD"]);
   128	    const closureAtStart = sourceClosureHash();
   129	    if (stage === "dev") {
   130	      if (p.frozen) throw new Error("the protocol is already frozen; the dev stage is for qualification before the freeze");
   131	      const plan = splitPlanOf(p);
   132	      const gen = generate(p, plan.dev, true);
   133	      writeText(join(out, "dev/qualification.json"), JSON.stringify(qualificationReport(p, gen), null, 1) + "\n");
   134	      console.log(`dev: ${gen.results.length} families, ${gen.variants.length} seed variants, ${Date.now() - t0} ms -> ${join(out, "dev/qualification.json")}`);
   135	      return 0;
   136	    }
   137	    if (stage === "confirm" || stage === "smoke") {
   138	      // `--exploratory` runs the confirm computation without the committed-freeze gate and marks every output EXPLORATORY: the test
   139	      // families are then not held out (their outcomes may already have been seen), so the run is a rehearsal, not a confirmation.
   140	      const exploratory = stage === "confirm" && opt.exploratory !== undefined;
   141	      if (amended) {
   142	        const freezeWorking = existsSync(freezePath) ? readFileSync(freezePath, "utf8") : null;
   143	        const refusal = amendmentRefusal({
   144	          protocol: p,
   145	          freezeWorking,
   146	          protocolWorking: readFileSync(protoPath, "utf8"),
   147	          amendmentWorking: existsSync(amendmentPath) ? readFileSync(amendmentPath, "utf8") : null,
   148	          committed: { amendment: committedText(amendmentPath), protocol: committedText(protoPath), freeze: committedText(freezePath) },
   149	          dirtyPaths: dirtyAtStart,
   150	          outDirNonEmpty: existsSync(out) && readdirSync(out).length > 0,
   151	          exploratory,
   152	        });
   153	        if (refusal) throw new Error(`refusing to confirm: ${refusal}`);
   154	      } else if (stage === "confirm" && !exploratory) {
   155	        const freezeWorking = existsSync(FREEZE) ? readFileSync(FREEZE, "utf8") : null;
   156	        const refusal = confirmRefusal(p, freezeWorking, { protocol: committedText(PROTOCOL), freeze: committedText(FREEZE), protocolWorking: readFileSync(PROTOCOL, "utf8"), freezeWorking: freezeWorking ?? "" });
   157	        if (refusal) throw new Error(`refusing to confirm: ${refusal}`);
   158	      }
   159	      if (stage === "confirm" && exploratory) {
   160	        const refusal = freezeRefusal(p, existsSync(FREEZE) ? readFileSync(FREEZE, "utf8") : null);
   161	        if (refusal) throw new Error(`refusing even to rehearse: ${refusal}`);
   162	      }
   163	      const gen = generate(p, p.families.map((f) => f.id), true);
   164	      const built = buildOutputs(p, gen);
   165	      for (const [name, text] of Object.entries(built.files)) writeText(join(out, name), text);
   166	      if (amended) writeText(join(out, ".gitignore"), "# Regenerable byte for byte from the committed amendment protocol; their hashes are in canonical.sha256.\nenvelopes.jsonl\npredictions.jsonl\n");
   167	      const meta = {
   168	        stage,
   169	        status: stage === "smoke" ? "SMOKE" : exploratory ? "EXPLORATORY" : "CONFIRM",
   170	        ...(exploratory ? { exploratoryReason: "run before the freeze was committed, with test outcomes already inspected; not a held-out confirmation" } : {}),
   171	        gitSha: gitShaAtStart,
   172	        gitDirty: dirtyAtStart.length > 0,
   173	        sourceClosureSha256: closureAtStart,
   174	        versions: RUN_VERSIONS,
   175	        protocolSha256: protocolHash(p),
   176	        ...(amended ? { amendment: { path: relative(root, amendmentPath), sha256: sha256Text(readFileSync(amendmentPath, "utf8")), protocolFile: relative(root, protoPath), freezeFile: relative(root, freezePath) } } : {}),
   177	        command: `deno run -A --no-lock tools/predict.ts ${Deno.args.join(" ")}`,
   178	        hardware: { cpu: cpus()[0]?.model ?? "unknown", cores: cpus().length, platform: platform(), arch: arch() },
   179	        wallMs: Date.now() - t0,
   180	        cellSteps: built.comparison.validNonNullYieldAndCost.totalCellSteps,
   181	        statement: "CPU only (sim-ref). No model call, no GPU, no network, no paid compute.",
   182	      };
   183	      writeText(join(out, "meta.json"), JSON.stringify(meta, null, 2) + "\n");
   184	      console.log(`${stage}: ${gen.results.length} families, ${built.files["envelopes.jsonl"].split("\n").length - 1} cases, ${Date.now() - t0} ms -> ${out}`);

     1	// Deterministic baseline proposers (PLAN.md, section 4). A proposer takes only an envelope and returns a prediction (never
     2	// outcomes, never another case's task). Constants are frozen in protocol.json and passed in; nothing here reads a file or a clock.
     3	import { RULES, type Prediction as RulesPrediction } from "../propose.ts";
     4	import { PREDICTION_VERSION, taskKey, type CensusPoint, type Direction3, type PredictionEnvelope } from "./envelope.ts";
     5	import { donorAllowed, type Split } from "./splits.ts";
     6	import type { Prediction, Probs } from "./validate.ts";
     7	
     8	export interface Proposer {
     9	  id: string;
    10	  /** baseline: a rule a model has to beat; control: a deliberately uninformed run; oracle: knows the mechanism, a dev diagnostic only. */
    11	  role: "baseline" | "control" | "oracle";
    12	  propose(env: PredictionEnvelope): Prediction;
    13	}
    14	
    15	export interface ProposerParams {
    16	  fixedRule: { lead: number };
    17	  numericTrend: { window: number; minRelPerMille: number; lead: number; mid: number; low: number };
    18	  v0Rules: { lead: number };
    19	  mechanismRule: { lead: number; lowDoseQuanta: number; scarceNutrient: number };
    20	}
    21	
    22	export function predictionOf(env: PredictionEnvelope, probs: Probs | null, reason: string): Prediction {
    23	  const base = { version: PREDICTION_VERSION, caseId: env.caseId, envelopeHash: env.envelopeHash, task: env.task, reason };
    24	  return probs ? { ...base, probs, abstain: false } : { ...base, abstain: true };
    25	}
    26	
    27	/** `lead` per-mille on `dir`, the rest split evenly between the other two (the first, in order up/down/none, takes any odd remainder). */
    28	export function leadProbs(dir: Direction3, lead: number): Probs {
    29	  const rest = 1000 - lead;
    30	  const half = Math.floor(rest / 2);
    31	  const out: Probs = { up: 0, down: 0, none: 0 };
    32	  const others = (["up", "down", "none"] as const).filter((c) => c !== dir);
    33	  out[dir] = lead;
    34	  out[others[0]] = rest - half;
    35	  out[others[1]] = half;
    36	  return out;
    37	}
    38	
    39	/** Largest-remainder rounding of non-negative weights to per-mille summing to exactly 1000. */
    40	export function toPerMille(w: Record<Direction3, number>): Probs {
    41	  const tot = w.up + w.down + w.none;
    42	  if (!(tot > 0)) throw new Error("weights must have a positive sum");
    43	  const cls = ["up", "down", "none"] as const;
    44	  const raw = cls.map((c) => (w[c] * 1000) / tot);
    45	  const fl = raw.map(Math.floor);
    46	  let left = 1000 - fl.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0);
    47	  const order = raw.map((r, i) => ({ i, f: r - fl[i] })).sort((a, b) => b.f - a.f || a.i - b.i);
    48	  for (const o of order) {
    49	    if (left <= 0) break;
    50	    fl[o.i]++;
    51	    left--;
    52	  }
    53	  return { up: fl[0], down: fl[1], none: fl[2] };
    54	}
    55	
    56	export const fixedRule = (k: ProposerParams["fixedRule"]): Proposer => ({
    57	  id: "fixedRule",
    58	  role: "baseline",
    59	  propose(env) {
    60	    const kind = env.task.op.kind;
    61	    const dir: Direction3 = kind === "feed" ? "up" : kind === "lesion" || kind === "drain" ? "down" : "none";
    62	    return predictionOf(env, leadProbs(dir, k.lead), `fixed rule: ${kind} -> ${dir}`);
    63	  },
    64	});
    65	
    66	/** The census-only trend: slope of the primary readout over the last `window` samples, extrapolated over the horizon, as a share of the current level. Ignores frames and the task. */
    67	export function trendDirection(census: readonly CensusPoint[], horizon: number, k: ProposerParams["numericTrend"]): Direction3 {
    68	  if (census.length < k.window + 1) return "none";
    69	  const last = census[census.length - 1], first = census[census.length - 1 - k.window];
    70	  const span = last.step - first.step;
    71	  if (span <= 0) return "none";
    72	  // change over the horizon, per-mille of the current level, in integer arithmetic.
    73	  const delta = (last.mass - first.mass) * horizon * 1000;
    74	  const bar = k.minRelPerMille * Math.max(last.mass, 1) * span;
    75	  return delta >= bar ? "up" : delta <= -bar ? "down" : "none";
    76	}
    77	function trendProbs(dir: Direction3, k: ProposerParams["numericTrend"]): Probs {
    78	  if (dir === "none") return { up: k.low, down: k.low, none: 1000 - 2 * k.low };
    79	  const other: Direction3 = dir === "up" ? "down" : "up";
    80	  const out: Probs = { up: 0, down: 0, none: k.mid };
    81	  out[dir] = k.lead;
    82	  out[other] = 1000 - k.lead - k.mid;
    83	  return out;
    84	}
    85	export const numericTrend = (k: ProposerParams["numericTrend"]): Proposer => ({
    86	  id: "numericTrend",
    87	  role: "baseline",
    88	  propose(env) {
    89	    const d = trendDirection(env.census, env.task.horizon, k);
    90	    return predictionOf(env, trendProbs(d, k), `census trend -> ${d}`);
    91	  },
    92	});
    93	
    94	/**
    95	 * The shuffled-evidence control: the numeric-trend rule run on a donor family's census. The donor is assigned by the frozen
    96	 * derangement; a donor from the same family, the same history (a later window of it) or another split is refused (throws), so a
    97	 * control can never be handed the case's own future.
    98	 */
    99	export function shuffledEvidence(
   100	  k: ProposerParams["numericTrend"],
   101	  donors: Readonly<Record<string, string>>,
   102	  envelopeOf: (familyId: string) => PredictionEnvelope | undefined,
   103	  splitOf: (familyId: string) => Split | undefined,
   104	): Proposer {
   105	  return {
   106	    id: "shuffledEvidence",
   107	    role: "control",
   108	    propose(env) {
   109	      const donorFamily = donors[env.familyId];
   110	      const donor = donorFamily === undefined ? undefined : envelopeOf(donorFamily);
   111	      if (!donor) throw new Error(`no donor envelope for family ${env.familyId}`);
   112	      const ok = donorAllowed(env, donor, splitOf);
   113	      if (!ok.ok) throw new Error(`donor refused for ${env.caseId}: ${ok.reason}`);
   114	      const d = trendDirection(donor.census, env.task.horizon, k);
   115	      return predictionOf(env, trendProbs(d, k), `donor census trend -> ${d}`);
   116	    },
   117	  };
   118	}
   119	
   120	export const priorMajority = (devPrior: Probs): Proposer => ({
   121	  id: "priorMajority",
   122	  role: "baseline",
   123	  propose: (env) => predictionOf(env, { ...devPrior }, "dev-split class frequencies"),
   124	});
   125	export const alwaysNone = (): Proposer => ({ id: "alwaysNone", role: "baseline", propose: (env) => predictionOf(env, { up: 0, down: 0, none: 1000 }, "always none") });
   126	export const abstainAll = (): Proposer => ({ id: "abstainAll", role: "baseline", propose: (env) => predictionOf(env, null, "abstains on every case") });
   127	
   128	/** The v0 rule proposer's directional/replay prediction mapped into the schema: 600 on the stated direction, the remainder on none (explicit, versioned). */
   129	export const RULES_MAP_VERSION = 1;
   130	export function fromRulesProposal(env: PredictionEnvelope, pred: RulesPrediction, lead: number): Prediction {
   131	  const probs: Probs = pred.kind === "directional" && pred.direction ? { up: 0, down: 0, none: 1000 - lead, [pred.direction]: lead } as Probs : { up: 0, down: 0, none: 1000 };
   132	  return predictionOf(env, probs, `rules-v0 (map v${RULES_MAP_VERSION}): ${pred.kind}${pred.direction ? " " + pred.direction : ""}`);
   133	}
   134	export const v0Rules = (k: ProposerParams["v0Rules"]): Proposer => ({
   135	  id: "v0Rules",
   136	  role: "baseline",
   137	  propose(env) {
   138	    // The v0 rule table (tools/lib/propose.ts RULES): feed and drain rows from the nutrient-held signals, lesion from the standing-body signals, control otherwise.
   139	    const kind = env.task.op.kind;
   140	    const row = kind === "feed" ? RULES.livingCells.low : kind === "drain" ? RULES.livingCells.high : kind === "lesion" ? RULES.lineages.high : RULES.lineages.low;
   141	    const pred: RulesPrediction = row.action === "control" ? { kind: "replay", statement: "replays" } : { kind: "directional", statement: `${row.action}`, direction: row.direction };
   142	    return fromRulesProposal(env, pred, k.lead);
   143	  },
   144	});
   145	
   146	/** Positive control, a dev diagnostic only: knows the fixture mechanisms (lesion centre occupancy from the frames, dose against the nutrient in the field). Never a baseline to beat. */
   147	export const mechanismRule = (k: ProposerParams["mechanismRule"]): Proposer => ({
   148	  id: "mechanismRule",
   149	  role: "oracle",
   150	  propose(env) {
   151	    const op = env.task.op;
   152	    const last = env.frames[env.frames.length - 1];
   153	    const cen = env.census[env.census.length - 1];
   154	    const block = (x: number, y: number): number => {
   155	      const w = env.config.tileW * env.config.tilesX;
   156	      const bs = Math.floor(w / last.side);
   157	      return last.mass[Math.floor(y / bs) * last.side + Math.floor(x / bs)];
   158	    };
   159	    let dir: Direction3 = "none";
   160	    if (op.kind === "lesion") dir = block(op.x!, op.y!) > 0 ? "down" : "none";
   161	    else if (op.kind === "feed") dir = (op.r! * op.r! * 3 * op.amount!) >= k.lowDoseQuanta ? "up" : "none";
   162	    else if (op.kind === "drain") dir = cen.freeNutrient >= k.scarceNutrient ? "down" : "none";
   163	    return predictionOf(env, leadProbs(dir, k.lead), `mechanism: ${op.kind} -> ${dir}`);
   164	  },
   165	});
   166	
   167	export const caseKey = (env: PredictionEnvelope): string => taskKey(env.task);
     1	// The host validator (PLAN.md, section 3). A prediction is accepted only if it is exactly the schema, restates the envelope's one
     2	// frozen task verbatim, and its task converts to operations the existing runner validator accepts. Invalid proposals are counted
     3	// and scored as non-answers; nothing is ever repaired. The sham ({kind:"none"}) is an explicit branch that maps to NO scheduled
     4	// operation (the existing replay control omits the schedule), never to a radius-0 lesion or a zero feed.
     5	import { cellCount, worldW } from "@bl/schema";
     6	import { interventionsError, validateInterventions, type ScheduledIntervention } from "@bl/runner";
     7	import { canonical } from "../propose.ts";
     8	import { CLASSES, ENVELOPE_VERSION, PREDICTION_VERSION, envelopeIntact, type Direction3, type PredictionEnvelope, type Task } from "./envelope.ts";
     9	
    10	export interface Probs {
    11	  up: number;
    12	  down: number;
    13	  none: number;
    14	}
    15	export interface Prediction {
    16	  version: number;
    17	  caseId: string;
    18	  envelopeHash: string;
    19	  task: Task;
    20	  probs?: Probs;
    21	  abstain: boolean;
    22	  reason?: string;
    23	}
    24	export interface Validation {
    25	  ok: boolean;
    26	  errors: string[];
    27	}
    28	
    29	const PRED_KEYS = ["version", "caseId", "envelopeHash", "task", "probs", "abstain", "reason"];
    30	/**
    31	 * True when `v` is plain JSON data all the way down: plain records and arrays whose every property is an own, enumerable data
    32	 * property (no getters, setters, symbols or hidden non-enumerable fields), so JSON.stringify sees exactly what the validator saw.
    33	 */
    34	function plainData(v: unknown, depth = 0): boolean {
    35	  if (v === null || typeof v === "string" || typeof v === "boolean") return true;
    36	  if (typeof v === "number") return Number.isFinite(v);
    37	  if (depth > 8 || typeof v !== "object") return false;
    38	  const isArr = Array.isArray(v);
    39	  if (!isArr) {
    40	    const proto = Object.getPrototypeOf(v);
    41	    if (proto !== Object.prototype && proto !== null) return false;
    42	  }
    43	  if (Object.getOwnPropertySymbols(v).length) return false;
    44	  for (const k of Reflect.ownKeys(v)) {
    45	    if (isArr && k === "length") continue;
     1	// The fixed evidence envelope, version 1 (PLAN.md, sections 1-2). One envelope per case: the world's config, a census series and K
     2	// registered frame samples, all at steps <= the fork step C, the menu of allowed intervention operations, and exactly one frozen
     3	// task. It holds no post-C data and no outcome. Identity is the SHA-256 of its canonical JSON (without the `envelopeHash` field).

     1	// Stability diagnostics for a frozen activity threshold (tools/calibrate.ts):
     2	// a seeded bootstrap interval, split-half agreement and per-run spread of
     3	// the pooled-neutral-activity quantile. Pure and host-agnostic (no Deno/Node
     4	// API) so it is unit-tested under vitest and used as-is from the Deno tool.
     5	import { quantile } from "./activity.ts";
     6	import { mean, sd } from "./stats.ts";
     7	
     8	/**
     9	 * Deterministic PRNG (mulberry32) -- small, fast, and reproducible: the same
    10	 * seed always produces the same draw sequence, so a calibration report's
    11	 * bootstrap interval is exactly reproducible from its recorded seed, not
    12	 * just "close" from run to run.
    13	 */
    14	export function mulberry32(seed: number): () => number {
    15	  let a = seed >>> 0;
    16	  return () => {
    17	    a |= 0;
    18	    a = (a + 0x6d2b79f5) | 0;
    19	    let t = Math.imul(a ^ (a >>> 15), 1 | a);
    20	    t = (t + Math.imul(t ^ (t >>> 7), 61 | t)) ^ t;
    21	    return ((t ^ (t >>> 14)) >>> 0) / 4294967296;
    22	  };
    23	}
    24	
    25	/** One eligible run's pooled lineage activities (tools/lib/bundle.ts's `(await activities(r)).tracker.allActivities()`), keyed by seed for split-half grouping. */
    26	export interface RunActivities {
    27	  seed: number;
    28	  activities: number[];

    32	export type Direction = "up" | "down";

    34	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    35	// Canonical JSON and identity.
    36	
    37	/** Canonical JSON: keys sorted (UTF-16 order) at every depth, no whitespace, numbers as JSON.stringify writes them. Throws on a non-finite number, undefined, or a non-plain object. */
    38	export function canonical(v: unknown, path = "$"): string {
    39	  if (v === null || typeof v === "boolean" || typeof v === "string") return JSON.stringify(v);
    40	  if (typeof v === "number") {
    41	    if (!Number.isFinite(v)) throw new Error(`canonical: ${path} is not a finite number`);
    42	    return JSON.stringify(v);
    43	  }
    44	  if (Array.isArray(v)) return `[${v.map((x, i) => canonical(x, `${path}[${i}]`)).join(",")}]`;
    45	  if (typeof v === "object" && (Object.getPrototypeOf(v) === Object.prototype || Object.getPrototypeOf(v) === null)) {
    46	    const o = v as Record<string, unknown>;
    47	    return `{${Object.keys(o)
    48	      .sort()
    49	      .map((k) => {
    50	        if (o[k] === undefined) throw new Error(`canonical: ${path}.${k} is undefined`);
    51	        return `${JSON.stringify(k)}:${canonical(o[k], `${path}.${k}`)}`;
    52	      })
    53	      .join(",")}}`;
    54	  }
    55	  throw new Error(`canonical: ${path} has unsupported type ${typeof v}`);
    56	}
    57	const sha = (text: string): string => sha256HexJS(new TextEncoder().encode(text));
    58	/** SHA-256 hex of a text (additive export for tools/lib/prediction, which hashes its envelopes the same way). */
    59	export const sha256Text = sha;
    60	export const bodySha256 = (body: ProposalBody): string => sha(canonical(body));
    61	export const proposalId = (body: ProposalBody): string => bodySha256(body).slice(0, 16);
    62	/** The SHA-256 of a run spec's canonical JSON: two rings that propose the same run are one proposal (the note, "Which rings"). */
   158	  }
   159	  return { ok: errors.length === 0, errors };
   160	}

   162	/** argmax of the stated probs, tie order none > down > up (derived, never submitted). */
   163	export function argmax(p: Probs): Direction3 {
   164	  let best: Direction3 = "none";
   165	  for (const c of ["none", "down", "up"] as const) if (p[c] > p[best]) best = c;
   166	  return best;
   167	}
